#2896. Qualys's 2027 Outlook: Achieving Federal Authorization Solidifies Public Sector Moat

This under-the-radar cybersecurity stock boasts a Superscore of 85 from our Hidden Gems Primary database, part of The Motley Fool's Moneyball Database system. Here's why.

By Robert Izquierdo – Updated Nov 25, 2026 at 3:53PM EST

When a large enterprise needs to lock down its entire digital footprint, it turns to Qualys (QLYS +2.47%). Picture a security officer managing thousands of servers, cloud containers, and remote endpoints, all of which represent a potential entry point for a breach. Qualys provides the unified platform that identifies those risks and automates the fix, turning a chaotic network into a defensible one. As of November 19, 2026, the stock trades at $192.51, having risen 39% over the past year.

Our proprietary Hidden Gems scoring system assigns Qualys an overall Superscore of 85 out of 100, placing it in the Strong category. The Superscore is an AI-powered score that evaluates a company's overall strength by combining financial performance, product market position, technological capabilities, leadership quality, and relative valuation. It represents the unification of all our scores into a single score for public companies, with five rating bands: Exceptional (90-100), Strong (75-89), Above Average (60-74), Average (40-59), and Cautious (0-39).

An 85 Superscore places the company in the Top ~4% of every company we score, which is ahead of roughly 96 out of every 100 companies we evaluate. This Superscore is one data-driven signal worth investigating, and this article pairs the reasons the score is high with the reasons it is not higher so you can weigh both sides before doing more work.

Qualys is a highly capital-efficient business, earning outsized profits on a small base of hard tangible assets. This efficiency allows the company to turn each point of revenue growth into strong returns, a trait the market often rewards with a premium valuation. While this efficiency is a clear advantage, it does not erase the risks posed by elevated valuation multiples and aggressive competition.

The Hidden Gems Superscore reflects The Motley Fool's proprietary AI-driven evaluation of a company across product, financial, leadership, and valuation pillars as of the article date and may change over time. Performance figures are point-in-time. Past performance does not guarantee future results.

You may want to keep researching before buying if...

The Superscore is one data-driven signal worth investigating, not a stand-alone buy recommendation, and you should weigh it against your own research, financial goals, and risk tolerance before acting.

Qualys is well positioned in the cybersecurity space. Organizations and government agencies are shifting to platforms that not only identify security vulnerabilities, but also fixes them. That is one of the company's key strengths, which has grown in importance now that hackers can employ artificial intelligence to quickly attack a system before an organization can respond. As a result, Qualys saw its federal government-related sales outperform expectations in Q3 as agencies consolidated spending onto its platform.

Moreover, while many software companies operate at a loss, Qualys is profitable with an impressive Q3 gross margin of 83%. It sports a fortress balance sheet, exiting the third quarter with total assets of $1.2 billion compared to total liabilities of $536.3 million, and of those liabilities, more than $425 million is deferred revenue, which will transition to sales after services are delivered.

Given the company's many strengths, Qualys is well positioned for years of growth ahead. Yet these factors have contributed to the stock's elevated valuation, with a trailing P/E ratio that's around a high point for the past year. This suggests now is not the best time to buy. Wait for the price to drop first before deciding to pick up shares.

#2891. Qualcomm vs. Sandisk: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

Sandisk's revenue surged over 270%, driven by demand for cloud memory, while Qualcomm is branching into automotive and satellite connectivity. Which chip stock holds up better?

By Brendan Coffey – Updated Nov 25, 2026 at 4:00PM EST

As mobile markets mature and artificial intelligence expands, investors are looking for the next big chip winner. Is Qualcomm (QCOM +1.30%) or Sandisk (SNDK -5.93%) the better choice for your 2027 portfolio?

Qualcomm provides the wireless connectivity and processors that power modern smartphones and connected vehicles. Sandisk specializes in the flash storage and memory solutions required for high-speed data processing in cloud environments. Both companies are essential to the global technology infrastructure, but they offer vastly different financial profiles for investors to consider.

As one of the most prominent semiconductor stocks, Qualcomm designs the high-performance computing and connectivity platforms found in premium handsets and industrial devices. The company relies on a small group of mobile handset customers, including Apple Inc. (AAPL +0.54%) and Samsung, for a significant portion of its revenue. Customer concentration like this adds a layer of risk to the business, particularly as Apple develops its own internal modem technology. To offset this, Qualcomm is expanding into automotive and data center markets while partnering with Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.07%) to provide radio platforms for the Starlink Mobile 5G network.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 27, 2026, revenue reached about $43.1 billion, representing a decrease of approximately 2.6% compared to the previous twelve-month period. Despite the slight revenue dip, the company reported a net income of roughly $13.6 billion for that time frame. Net margin for the year was about 32%, showing the company's ability to maintain high profitability even during a period of slowing top-line growth.

The company maintains a solid balance sheet, with a debt-to-equity ratio of approximately 0.5x as of its September 2026 balance sheet. This ratio compares total debt to shareholder equity, indicating the company maintains a manageable level of borrowing. Its current ratio, which measures the ability to cover short-term debts with short-term assets, stands at nearly 1.9x. For the fiscal year ended Sept. 27, 2026, free cash flow was nearly $8.7 billion. Note that stock-based compensation (SBC) represented roughly 31% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

Sandisk operates as a major force in the flash memory industry, serving the massive data center, PC, and mobile device markets. The company provides the hardware necessary for storing and moving the vast amounts of data used by modern artificial intelligence systems. Unlike many chip peers, its sales are highly diverse, with no single customer accounting for more than 10% of revenue in the most recent fiscal year. This broad base of OEM and channel customers helps reduce the impact of any single partnership ending.

In the 12 months ended Oct. 2, 2026, revenue reached about $29 billion, a significant growth of approximately 272% over the prior year. This rapid expansion was accompanied by a net income of roughly $19.4 billion. The surge in sales and earnings reflects the intense demand for high-capacity memory solutions in the cloud computing sector. The company achieved a net margin of nearly 67% during this period, highlighting the strong demand for its memory technology.

As of July 2026, the company reported a debt-to-equity ratio of 0.0x, indicating that total liabilities do not exceed shareholder equity and its reliance on debt is minimal. Its current ratio was nearly 2.3x, suggesting a comfortable cushion for meeting its short-term financial obligations. In the fiscal year ended July 3, 2026, free cash flow reached nearly $11.5 billion. This robust cash generation allows the company to reinvest heavily in manufacturing facilities and next-generation storage research.

Qualcomm faces significant risks from its revenue concentration among a small number of handset manufacturers. The company expects Apple will transition to its own internally developed modems in the near future, which could impact sales. Furthermore, heavy exposure to the Chinese market makes the company vulnerable to shifting trade policies and national security regulations. Its reliance on a fabless model also means it depends on third-party foundries like Taiwan Semiconductor and Samsung for leading-edge production capacity.

Sandisk carries operational risks due to its heavy reliance on joint ventures with Kioxia for memory production. Its manufacturing facilities are geographically concentrated in Japan and Malaysia, leaving the supply chain open to disruption from natural disasters or geopolitical conflict. The memory industry is also notoriously cyclical, with intense competition from Nvidia Corp. (NVDA -3.94%), Western Digital (WDC -0.88%), and Micron Technology (MU -4.83%). The company must also manage the complexities associated with its separation from Western Digital, including potential tax liabilities.

Sandisk appears significantly cheaper based on future earnings estimates, while Qualcomm maintains a slightly higher premium for its diversified connectivity portfolio.

Valuation metrics include those sourced from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from those of other data providers.

Qualcomm's mobile phone business has been hammered this year, as weak Chinese demand and AI-driven memory shortages have caused handset-derived sales to fall below prior-year levels. Yet the AI boom has an upside for Qualcomm.

At the company's June 2026 investor day, Qualcomm raised its non-handset revenue target to more than $40 billion by 2029, nearly double the prior target of $22 billion. Qualcomm is reportedly partnering with OpenAI to develop smartphone processors with advanced AI capabilities. Analysts see this partnership as a positive for Qualcomm since it would enable advanced agentic AI on smartphone platforms. It would also diminish the business risk of Apple developing its own chips and dropping Qualcomm as a supplier.

Meanwhile, Sandisk shares have undergone remarkable appreciation since its spin-off from Western Digital created the firm as a stand-alone entity in February 2025. Shares went from $49 to more than $2,300 by the middle of 2026. Shares have since undergone an 8-for-1 split and seen a significant retracement of gains.

But the share strength shows just how strong the flash memory market has been for Sandisk. During periods of tight supply and strong market pricing, the company's profit margins can be robust, as they have been during this AI-driven market. Historically, though, high memory prices and low supply are followed by periods of high supply and low prices, which are a risk to Sandisk investors in the long run.

Still, the growth is impressive. Revenue should get near $50 billion 2027, more than double 2026, with Wall Street seeing more than $60 billion in sales in 2028. Net income should triple in 2027, to $34.9 billion, and grow more modestly in 2028.

The choice here is simply: Sandisk is booming and comes at a bargain-basement valuation while Qualcomm's expected improvement is still speculative at the moment. There is risk to Sandisk that one may be buying at the peak of the memory cycle, but it's clearly the better bet for 2027.

#2887. The Best Dividend Stocks to Buy With $5,000 in November

Here's a reliable high-yield stock, a dividend growth stock, and a turnaround story for you to consider.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 25, 2026 at 4:01PM EST

November is drawing to a close, but you still have some time to put money to work in dividend stocks. Say, $5,000, which could give you a toehold in a reliable high-yield stock like Federal Realty (FRT +1.50%), a dividend growth stock like Visa (V +1.98%), or a turnaround story like Pfizer (PFE +0.16%). Here's a look at each one and why, depending on how you prefer to invest, it could be a good place for your cash before the month is over.

Federal Realty is a bit unique in its strip-mall property niche, owning only around 100 assets. It is focused on being a sharpshooter, and its aim is very good. The proof of that is that it is the only Dividend King real estate investment trust (REIT). You don't string together 50+ annual dividend increases by accident; it requires a strong business model that is executed well in both good times and bad.

Federal Realty's approach is actually pretty simple. It buys properties surrounded by lots of wealthy customers. Then it redevelops the assets to ensure they are attractive to both consumers and tenants, including carefully curating the tenant list. When a property reaches full value, the REIT sells it and starts again with a new asset. That approach isn't particularly shocking, but it is hard to execute well. Federal Realty's dividend track record shows it consistently executes well.

You can find higher-yielding REITs pretty easily, but there's only one REIT that can claim to be a Dividend King. For conservative dividend investors, Federal Realty's well-above-market 4.2% yield should help you sleep well at night, even when the market gets turbulent. A $5,000 investment will let you buy 45 shares.

Visa's tiny 0.75% yield probably won't interest you if you are looking for high-yield stocks. That said, the dividend has grown by more than 370% over the past decade, which is a shockingly large number. If you don't need the income today and can set this stock to dividend reinvest, that kind of dividend growth could easily leave you with a robust income stream when you retire.

Visa is one of the world's largest payment processors. It has long benefited from the ongoing shift from paper currency to cards. It only collects a small percentage of each transaction it processes, but when a company handles 74.2 billion transactions in a single quarter (up 10% year over year), the numbers start to add up. And since the world is increasingly moving toward digital transactions, where paper money isn't an option, Visa is likely to have years of growth ahead. The stock isn't cheap, but given the growth opportunity here, it is probably still worth a look for long-term investors. A $5k investment will let you buy 12 shares.

Pfizer, an iconic drug maker, has a huge 6.2% yield. The average yield on pharma stocks is just 1.3%. The core problem Pfizer is facing is major patent expirations in the next few years, with Eliquis losing protection in 2028 and Vyndamax in 2031. It is a very real problem, but Pfizer is actively working on its pipeline to offset the revenue hit. Only, drug development doesn't work on a schedule, even though patent expirations do.

Investors clearly fear the worst, which could include a dividend cut. However, Pfizer's cash flow still covers its dividend, and it has over $14 billion of cash on its balance sheet. Moreover, the company continues to stand behind not only supporting the dividend at its current level, but growing it over time. Given the company's long history of success in the pharma sector, turnaround investors should give it a look. And even if there were a 50% dividend cut, which is probably a realistic worst-case scenario, the yield would still be over 3%. Five thousand dollars will buy you 178 shares.

No two investors are alike, which is why Wall Street is such an exciting place. When it comes to dividends, you can find boring and reliable dividend stocks like Federal Realty. You can find dividend growth stocks, such as Visa. And you can find turnaround stocks like Pfizer that come with ultra-high yields. Each one is worth a deep dive today.

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2877. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2874. Jensen Huang's Nvidia return shows the U.S.-China AI race is about more than chips

BEIJING — Jensen Huang's return to Nvidia as its CEO underscores how the U.S.-China artificial intelligence race is not only about semiconductors, but tech talent.

"We have to protect our geniuses, and he's clearly a genius," Donald Trump told CNBC about his intervention to bring Huang back. If not, Trump warned, the founder of the AI chip giant could hypothetically have taken a job in China.

The concern reflects how Chinese AI companies have been narrowing the gap with their U.S. counterparts, not just in models, but now in engineering and research talent. China has surpassed the U.S. by share of global AI talent in just three years, according to Carnegie Endowment research.

Nvidia "was shaken without a single chip or model changing," said Winston Ma, an AI investor and adjunct professor of law at New York University. "Whoever builds and keeps that human layer will hold the rest of the stack."

Huang resigned Nov. 12 after reports of an affair with a woman U.S. intelligence reportedly believes has links to Chinese intelligence. He returned on Nov. 23, news which sent Nvidia shares surging.

More than 30 years ago, Huang founded Nvidia as a gaming graphics chip company. The semiconductors gradually became used to mine cryptocurrencies, and now, training cutting-edge AI models.

Huang has more advanced tech coming, Trump told CNBC's Eamon Javers on Monday.

"He's got other chips in his brain that nobody else knows about, including me, that nobody else understands," Trump said.

"Jensen could take the next plane to China, and I guarantee you, they would give him whatever he wanted, and then and then they automatically have, you know, all of the things," Trump said. He acknowledged there was "never even a suggestion" that the Nvidia founder was recruited by China.

Trump returned to the U.S. over the weekend from Shenzhen, China, where he met Chinese President Xi Jinping. Earlier in the month, turmoil around Huang's future at Nvidia had gripped global attention.

For Huang's supporters, talent was a critical part of the argument, said Damien Ma, director and Maurice R. Greenberg Director's Chair of Carnegie China.

As the incident has increased scrutiny on the people involved in the U.S.-China tech race, Ma said he's closely watching whether either country restricts AI engineers from leaving. While he expects limited policy changes, other countries such as Singapore and South Korea could benefit from an inflow of AI talent, he added.

Already, many companies are using a mixed-market approach, given engineers in the U.S. make two or three times as much as their Chinese counterparts due in part to foreign exchange rates and AI company valuations.

In the U.S., "you need to have a lot of money to recruit them," said Alex Lu, founder of LSY Consulting — and that can get expensive.

Lu cited the founder of a U.S. consumer research firm, who opted to hire engineers based in China after visiting the country.

It's an anecdote that's frequently repeated by analysts, sometimes with U.S.-trained Chinese staff based in Singapore.

Chinese companies are "very, very aggressive in getting good talent from the U.S.," said Tidalwave Solutions Partner Rachel Wu, who recruits for large Chinese tech companies and startups.

She also said a Meta engineer in Canada took an offer from a Chinese AI startup for a salary of 1.4 million yuan ($210,000), in addition to stock options.

"There is some truth to Trump's comments given Jensen's knowledge of the AI industry and supply chain," said Lian Jye Su, a chief analyst of Omdia. "It would be unwise for the U.S. to let this saga continue to affect its AI ambition, especially when that ambition includes slowing down Chinese progress in AI."

However, Rui Ma, founder of advisory Tech Buzz China, said Huang's background of being a founder makes it more likely that he would have started his own company, rather than taking a managerial role at one.

She also noted Huang's "deep belief in America" and laws that would block him from using his access to TSMC and other suppliers to help a Chinese company.

The U.S. has restricted China's access to advanced Nvidia semiconductors over the last four years. But in early 2025, DeepSeek upended assumptions that chip restrictions could keep Chinese AI capabilities well behind that of the U.S.

A few years ago, only the U.S. and Singapore had the kind of AI talent that Chinese companies were looking for, Wu said. "Today we see the gap has been filled," she said, adding the supply of local talent has increased in the last two to three years.

"The demand is huge," Wu said, saying one ex-Alibaba AI engineering candidate with seven years of experience had more than five offers.

Switching between companies can result in a 25% to 30% pay bump, Wu said. She expects the trend to persist for another two years, although the increase in compensation may not be as high.

Still, restricted access to advanced AI chips plays a role in where the best researchers choose to work.

It's a question of "where I can do my further research and do I have enough resources to do my research," said Lu.

He said industry research suggests "China is becoming a really important pool for talent, while the U.S. is paying 80% of the money."

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2870. Pistons vs Magic final score: Detroit’s dominant defense shows up with Cade Cunningham sidelined

The Goin to Work Pistons showed up tonight. Detroit dispatched the Orlando Magic 104-86. This was another statement game. It doesn't matter who plays; defense travels. Detroit is now 2-1 in NBA Cup play with a plus-31 point differential. Orlando felt Detroit's defensive wrath, holding Orlando to 14 second-quarter points. They were everywhere.

Cade Cunningham was a late scratch, but the next man steps up here. Both teams struggled to put the ball in the hoop, but Detroit generated better shots. They swung the ball from side to side on plenty of possessions. The shot-making results weren't always there, but their process was solid. It was probably more than solid considering they were down three starters tonight.

Ausar Thompson and Daniss Jenkins picked up more on-ball reps. Jenkins had a 15-point and 10-assist double-double. Thompson continues to grow as a playmaker for others as he added seven more assists tonight.

Ausar seems much more sure of himself this season. Even the moments he turns down shots to find a teammate are telling — everything seems planned.

Ausar wasn't the only Piston with an all-around A performance. Kevin Huerter finished with 13-5-6. He worked the off-ball screen here, slightly froze the big, and threw an accurate lob to Paul Reed.

Isaiah Joe led the team with 21 points. He went 5-for-11 from trey. Joe showed off the range and confidence. He pressured the ball on the other end. Shooters competing on defense, and not being must-hide defenders, is such a plus. Joe is picking it up.

Ron Holland has success from deep. He buried two 3s. Detroit shot it well as a team despite their low point total. They went 15-for-36 from deep compared to Orlando's 12-for-36. John Collins, Woo, and Elijah Harkless (he made some momentum-swinging ones) all hit two 3s.

Detroit knocked down 3s but kept Orlando from doing anything. Franz Wagner had been on a hot streak until he ran into this defense (29 PPG over the last 10). He (20 pts) and Desmond Bane (21 pts) put numbers on the board, but it wasn't easy. Those two saw a crowd every time they drove the ball. Wagner started spamming push shots that felt automatic, but Detroit held him in check.

Anthony Black was a non-factor offensively tonight. That's a testament to Detroit's D, as he's been rolling all year. The Magic missed so many shots at the rim. They were initially dominating Detroit on the offensive glass, but those missed shots eventually turned into Detroit run-outs.

The Pistons also got their hands on so many Magic passes tonight. They made Orlando creators play with multiple hands in their faces and trusted their closeouts to disrupt "open" Orlando 3s. Holding a team under 90 in 2026 is some Goin to Work stuff. Hats off to J.B. Bickerstaff for having this defense ready to go at all times.

Detroit's final group play cup game is Friday versus the flaming hot Tyler Herro and the Milwaukee Bucks. Detroit could still win the group even after losing to the Toronto Raptors last Friday. They might need some help, but must start by handling their business with the Bucks.

#2869. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2866. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2862. China’s Xiamen Airlines to buy 50 Boeing jets in $7.4 billion deal

China Southern Airlines said in a filing late Tuesday that its subsidiary Xiamen Airlines had agreed to purchase 50 Boeing 737-8 aircraft with a combined catalog price of approximately $7.4 billion.

The announcement comes after President Donald Trump told CNBC on Monday that he expected China to purchase another 250 Boeing aircraft, following his meeting with Chinese President Xi Jinping at the APEC summit last week.

"By the way, I sold 250 Boeings to China, and I think they're going to do another 250," Trump said.

In May, China approved an initial purchase of 200 Boeing aircraft for Chinese airlines following Trump's visit to Beijing, marking the country's first commitment to purchase U.S.-made Boeing aircraft since 2017, according to the White House.

It was not immediately clear whether Xiamen Airlines' latest purchase was part of that commitment.

China Southern, which owns a 55% stake in Xiamen Airlines, said the actual purchase price would be lower than the combined list price because of discounts offered by Boeing. The company did not disclose the final purchase price, citing confidentiality provisions in the agreement.

The aircraft are scheduled to be delivered in batches between 2031 and 2034, with the purchase financed through a combination of internal funds and external financing, the filing showed. The transaction remains subject to shareholder and relevant Chinese government approvals.

China Southern said the purchase would support Xiamen Airlines' planned relocation to the new Xiamen Xiang'an International Airport and the expansion of its route network.

The agreement follows the group's recent purchase agreement for 137 Airbus A320neo-family aircraft, the filing showed. China Southern said sourcing aircraft from different manufacturers would help diversify its fleet and improve operational flexibility.

Other Asian carriers, including Philippine Airlines and Malaysia Aviation Group, have also announced Boeing aircraft orders this month.

#2861. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2859. Trump Account contributions from friends and family pass $1 billion mark, Treasury says

Families and friends have collectively contributed more than $1 billion to Trump Accounts since the July 4 launch, bringing total deposits to more than $9 billion, the U.S. Department of the Treasury said Tuesday in an announcement exclusively provided to CNBC.

The $1 billion in Trump Account deposits come from more than 3 million individuals, according to the agency. That total does not include the government's seed money or philanthropic gifts from donors such as Michael and Susan Dell.

"The American people are investing in Trump Accounts with a generosity that represents the finest instincts of our national character," U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said in a written statement.

"Trump Accounts are a transformative government program for American children. It is the largest government start-up in American history," Internal Revenue Service CEO Frank Bisignano said in the statement.

Also known as 530A accounts, Trump Accounts are open to any U.S. child under 18 with a Social Security number.

Parents, guardians, grandparents and others can collectively contribute up to $5,000 per child per year in after-tax dollars up until the year before the beneficiary turns 18. The annual contribution limit is indexed for inflation after 2027.

Children born from 2025 through 2028 may receive a one-time $1,000 deposit from the Treasury. Other funds may also be available, depending on certain criteria.

Most recently, Snowflake chairman Frank Slootman and his wife, Brenda, announced they would give $1,000 to every child in Montana born in 2023 and 2024.

The Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump Accounts to hold stock donations, a move meant to pave the way for more philanthropic gifts, according to Bisignano.

These rules make it more attractive for donors, according to Ben Henry-Moreland, a certified financial planner with advisor platform Kitces.com.

By gifting stocks, wealthy founders and shareholders can avoid the capital gains taxes that would otherwise be triggered by selling appreciated stock and donating the cash proceeds. However, it also means some children's wealth accumulation could be tied to the performance of individual companies.

The Trump Accounts milestone comes as more billionaires and companies pledge support to the investment account for kids.

In an exclusive interview with CNBC's "Squawk on the Street," Bessent on Nov. 20 announced an Anthropic co-founder would make a "large gift" to Trump Accounts over the next few weeks.

A person briefed on the announcement confirmed that the Anthropic co-founder Bessent referred to is Tom Brown and that it is part of his broader philanthropic plan. The person asked not to be named because the matter is private.

"This is the perfect vehicle to donate to the children of America, and they will receive 100% of the proceeds," Bessent said.

Elon Musk wrote in an X post that he plans to give every kid in America a share of SpaceX sometime in 2027, which experts say may come via Trump Accounts.

In a separate funding announcement, SpaceX President Gwynne Shotwell said she would donate around 2 million shares of her and her husband's SpaceX stock to the accounts.

On Monday, Altimeter Capital CEO Brad Gerstner, founder of Invest America and an architect of Trump Accounts, told CNBC's "Halftime Report" that more large-scale donations may be in the pipeline.

"I want everybody to own shares of Nvidia, in Anthropic, in OpenAI," he said. "We created 70 million stock accounts — free stock accounts — for every single citizen under the age of 18 to do just that, and we have huge announcements coming."

#2855. Celtics survive late scare in NBA Cup, beat Wizards 131-116

The Boston Celtics traveled to Washington to face the Wizards in their third game of the NBA Cup Group C on Tuesday night. After a close first quarter, Boston exploded in the second quarter with 46 points as they cruised to large lead at halftime, but Washington completely outplayed Boston in the second half as they raced back into the contest. Boston managed to hang on to the win late with a 131-116 victory with Jayson Tatum scoring 41 points on the night.

For Boston, they had a new-look starting five with Derrick White, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, Sam Hauser, and Neemias Queta. Head coach Joe Mazzulla opted to move away from Jordan Walsh in the starting back court and Mitchell Robinson (illness) remained out for Boston.

The Wizards started Trae Young, Kyshawn George, AJ Dybantsa, Anthony Davis, and Alexandre Sarr. The 2026 NBA number 1 pick, AJ Dybantsa, hit the first points of the game with a two-point jumper. Boston hit their first 4 shots of the game as they got out to an early 9-2 lead. AJ Dybantsa scored the first 4 points for Washington against his favorite team growing up.

Neemias Queta had two early lob dunks and 8 early points for Boston against the double-big lineup of Washington. Tatum and George had one bucket each, but it was all Neemias Queta to start the game for the visitors.

Baylor Scheierman and Payton Pritchard were the first Celtics off the bench for Boston. Ron Harper Jr. also checked into the game for first-quarter minutes for Boston as the coaching staff seemingly searching for a spark after 3 straight road losses.

Chris Cenac Jr. checked into the game late in the quarter and provided some defensive hustle for Boston. Payton Pritchard nailed a deep logo triple for his first score of the game. Both teams traded buckets in a four-minute stretch to finish off the quarter well. After a high-octane quarter of basketball, Boston took a 34-28 lead into the second quarter.

Baylor Scheierman bailed out the C's down the stretch — he had a pair of 3-pointers and 8 points in the last 2 and a half minutes of the game. Boston hung on despite Washington pushing them all the way.

The Boston Celtics will now travel home after 4 straight road games to host the Atlanta Hawks on Friday 27th of November at 8 pm EST. A win would clinch Group C and a close loss would most likely give them a wild card berth.

#2853. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2850. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2848. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

#2845. Utah drops criminal charges against Kalshi founders after company suspended operations in state

Utah on Tuesday dismissed criminal charges against Kalshi and its two co-founders following the decision by the prediction market platform on Monday to suspend operations in the state.

The state originally filed the 14 criminal charges against the company and founders Tarek Mansour and Luana Lopes Lara on Oct. 28, claiming that Kalshi operated an illegal gambling operation in the state.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission — the federal regulator for prediction markets which views itself as the sole body that can develop rules over the growing industry — sued Utah the day before the state announced its charges.

Mansour and Lara were slated to appear in a Utah district court on Dec. 4, according to court documents.

However, Kalshi suspended operations in Utah on Monday, citing legal uncertainty. Utah Attorney General Derek Brown celebrated the decision in a statement announcing the move to dismiss the state's charges.

"Every company in this space should take note," he said. "If they promote, support, or enable gambling, we will hold them accountable."

Brown's office noted in the statement that dismissal is without prejudice, meaning if Kalshi were to begin operations again in the state the charges could be refiled.

"Good faith jurisdictional disagreements should never be criminalized," Kalshi's general counsel Yaakov Roth said in a post on X. "We stand ready to work with State AGs on reasonable interim measures until courts resolve the dispute over who regulates prediction markets."

While Kalshi faces legal setbacks in Utah, it faced some relief in New York on Monday when a state judge denied an emergency temporary restraining order against the company to halt its operations in the jurisdiction. The court will hear arguments on the matter again in January.

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2842. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2838. Dell earnings beat on AI strength, company authorizes $25 billion buyback

Dell reported third-quarter earnings on Tuesday that topped Wall Street expectations for earnings and sales, and provided a strong guide for the fourth quarter.

Here's how the chipmaker did versus LSEG consensus estimates:

Dell said that it expected earnings of $8.40 per share on revenue of about $56.3 billion in the current quarter. Analysts polled by LSEG were looking for $7.50 per share on $53.61 billion in revenue.

The company's overall sales more than doubled on an annual basis during the third quarter, driven by AI server demand.

The company's largest line is its infrastructure solutions group, or ISG, which makes AI servers and systems, often based around AI chips from companies like Nvidia and AMD.

ISG sales during the quarter were $41.97 billion, topping an LSEG estimate of $35.27 billion. Of that, $22 billion was for AI-optimized servers, up 291% on an annual basis. ISG now accounts for 85% of Dell's total revenue.

"We are now a different company, with Infrastructure Solutions Group driving the vast majority of our profit and growth," said Jeff Clarke, Dell's vice chairman and COO, in a statement. He said that Dell has 7,500 customers buying AI servers.

In prepared remarks, Clarke said that Dell has a competitive advantage over other computer equipment makers because it allows customers to get their computers working within hours of delivery.

"That allows customers to convert backlog into revenue sooner and more reliably," Clarke said in the prepared remarks published by Dell.

The company also raised its forecast for AI-servers in the coming fiscal year to $78 billion, versus $74 billion expected. The company says it has a backlog of $132.3 billion in AI server orders it is working through.

"Every AI order is vetted for access to capital, end customer quality, trade and export compliance and access to land and power," Clarke said on an earnings call with analysts.

The company also sells laptops and PCs, in a group it calls CSG, or client services group. It reported $12.89 billion in sales, which was below LSEG expectations of $14.33 billion.

The overall PC market has been getting whacked of late, with some estimates calling the lull the worst ever, as rising memory prices have led to higher device prices and consumers are choosing to hang on to their old laptops longer.

Dell said that it had authorized $25 billion in share repurchases, and that it had spent $4.9 billion in the third quarter on share repurchases and dividends.

Dell shares are up over 135% so far in 2026.

#2836. Kawhi Leonard is back on the (practice) court: will his return be enough?

The Raptors have released another brief statement outlining Kawhi Leonard's return to playing NBA basketball, and while there are positive signs emerging, little can be confirmed about when the forward will be back on the court.

"He has begun individual on-court workouts following treatment to address left knee swelling," stated the press release issued earlier today, November 24th. Leonard has not seen the court for more than one month, last having suited up in game two for the Raptors on October 23rd in a win for Toronto against the Washington Wizards.

Without Kawhi, the Raptors have been in free-fall, punctuated by a six-game losing streak earlier in November. Some of these were close, against good teams; the Raptors would develop a lead, then give it away in the second half, breaking the hearts of Toronto fans who tuned in to watch. With his clutch genes and scoring prowess, Leonard's presence on the team would have been sure to change the outcome of some of these games, but the lack of his presence has revealed both flaws, and upsides with this roster.

The Raptors roster, as currently constructed, is not that different from what it was last year. Regardless of having Leonard or Brandon Ingram at small-forward, these two players were signed for the same job: take offensive responsibilities off of Scottie Barnes' shoulders so that he can focus on playmaking and defence.

All together, a sound idea for roster construction. But the state of the starters around the pairs in both years? Less than ideal. The Raptors are currently ranked 24th in three-pointers made, and are shooting 33.8% from deep this season. A big reason for this is because this year, 3 of 5 Raptors starers are not considered good three point shooters, those being Scottie Barnes, Jakob Poeltl, and RJ Barrett, while the two perceived marksmen, Immanuel Quickley, and Kawhi Leonard, have both been unreliable.

While the Raptors, ironically, have quite a few good three point shooting players or prospects coming off their bench, the Raps' starting lineup, the players who they have committed the most money to, are either non-shooters, in a shooting slump, or injured. While a lack of clutch play and poor defence is also certainly to blame for the Raptors' losses, being able to buy a 3 certainly would help the Raptors, especially in the fourth quarter of games.

It's not 2006 anymore, and the Raptors need to get with the times. Giving Ja'Kobe Walter more minutes is a step in the right direction, but with his certain relegation to the bench when Kawhi Leonard returns, the Raptors' rotations need to consistently sport players who can make long range shots.

As a whole, the Raptors have added Leonard to a problematic roster which in many respects, he was meant to, and seemed potentially able to fix. But, succeeding with Leonard always comes contingent with being able to survive the regular periods of absence he takes as a result of health issues. And the Raptors have shown themselves largely unable to do so.

One of the lone bright spots in the Raptors' season thus far has been the ascension of Scottie Barnes to hitherto unforeseen levels. Barnes has been thrust into a primary offensive creation role and has taken to it unexpectedly well. As of November 24th, Barnes is averaging almost 25 points per game and a career high in steals. He's doing it playing an antiquated style of basketball, too, barely making any threes, and living off of midrange buckets.

Barnes has never had a higher profile, nor has he ever been more fun to watch on the offensive side of the ball.

Allen Graves, the Santa Clara rookie drafted 19th overall, has done a lot to increase his volume from three, and had his first signature game, with a 20 point, 10 rebound performance to help the Raptors put away the Detroit Pistons. While it's unlikely for that same level of play to extend the rest of the season, Graves has shown that he's capable of doing what he was drafted to do, and could be the next developmental success story for the Raptors.

Getting Leonard back to pair with Barnes, Graves, and Collin Murray-Boyles gives the Raptors a disgusting forward rotation to lean on heavily, and Leonard's offensive abilities will likely be able to drag the Raptors back into a positive win/loss ratio. But, ideally, Leonard should not be dragging dead weight behind him. The Raptors, as an organization, need to take account of the state of their team without Kawhi Leonard. He won't always be available to bail out this roster, and when he isn't, it is irresponsible coaching and management to allow the roster to go on to lose games in the manner that they are currently losing them in.

Leonard is not being paid to help rookies ascend and find their place in the NBA. This is team headlined by professional, veteran players, and Leonard or no-Leonard, they should look like it on the basketball court.

The Raptors will give more information on Kawhi's health in a week. Stay frosty.

An official website of the United States Government
