#2742. Even the price of toys is on the rise. How Americans are scaling back holiday plans

As Amanda Brecht planned how her family would spend Thanksgiving this year, she had to make some difficult decisions.

Because of high gas prices, the 30-year-old decided she wouldn't drive across her home state of Michigan to celebrate with extended family. Instead, Brecht and her husband will spend the holiday with just their three kids. And with food prices rising, they'll be skipping appetizers and serving turkey breast instead of a whole bird.

"Thanksgiving is definitely going to look different this year," Brecht said. "Money doesn't grow on trees. You can only do so much."

With inflation accelerating, Americans like Brecht will see higher prices almost everywhere they look this holiday season. At a time of year synonymous with food, gifts and big spending, many consumers are instead pinching pennies, paring back their plans and making tough choices about how they'll celebrate.

Some are raiding their savings accounts and running up debt on credit cards. They're reporting downbeat outlooks about the economy in surveys. And they've become more selective with spending as inflationary pressures mount.

"Families are facing some tough choices this year," said Alexander Smith, an associate professor who researches behavioral economics at the Worcester Polytechnic Institute. "They might have to cut back on holiday spending in whatever way that they can."

A classic Thanksgiving feast will be harder to pull off as grocery prices rise.

The price of a 10-person Thanksgiving dinner increased 6% to $58.63 in 2026, according to an annual survey from the American Farm Bureau Federation. Faith Parum, the organization's deputy chief economist, said the price increase was a result of higher costs for labor and transportation.

Grocers including Walmart and Aldi have gained traction on social media by offering Thanksgiving meal bundles as a value-focused workaround to higher prices. Walmart's offering promises to feed 10 for $35, and includes a turkey and ingredients to make mashed potatoes, green bean casserole, cranberries, marshmallow-topped sweet potatoes and a pumpkin pie.

Still, some consumers will feel disappointed by having less choice in their menus due to higher prices, according to Joe Bruseulas, chief economist at tax firm RSM.

"We're missing the point here if all we're doing is focusing on the fact that Walmart will sell you a homogeneous dinner meal" at a discount, Bruseulas said. "People feel horrible that they have to go to that because the price of everything has just risen so significantly over the past five years."

Inflation is affecting more than Thanksgiving staples like turkey or stuffing. Food prices on average have jumped around 24% over the last five years, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics. Some items have seen even sharper increases: Coffee and ground beef, for example, have each climbed by more than 40% in the same period despite pulling back from recent highs.

At the same time, millions of Americans lost SNAP benefits under policy changes included in President Donald Trump's "big beautiful bill." Food banks across the U.S. have seen foot traffic skyrocket this year as the reduction in federal nutrition aid coincides with rising prices in grocery aisles.

The Salvation Army said some of its food pantries in October provided double or triple the amount of groceries than they did in the same month a year ago. Demand is now so high that some food banks in Feeding America's national network are worried about running out of supplies.

"We want to make sure that our food banks are as full as they possibly can be going into the holiday season," said Linda Nageotte, Feeding America's president and operations chief. "Far too many of them have empty shelves."

High energy prices as a result of the war with Iran are also affecting Americans' holiday plans.

Slightly fewer Americans will travel at least 50 miles around the Thanksgiving holiday in 2026, marking the first year-over-year decline since the country was in lockdown from the pandemic in 2020, according to AAA. Stacey Barber, a vice president at the auto association, attributed this year's pullback in part to higher fuel costs.

AAA said that about nine out of every 10 Thanksgiving travelers will do so by car. While gas prices have come down from a May peak, they are still up about 18% year over year and could set a new record for the month of November, according to the organization's data.

Travelers are paying about 31% and 23% more for roundtrip domestic airfare around Thanksgiving and Christmas, respectively, than they did a year ago, according to booking platform Hopper. Airlines have blamed the ticket price hikes on rising jet fuel costs in the wake of the war.

"There will be a significant shell shock when they see the price of traveling," said Hayley Berg, lead economist at Hopper.

Berg said price increases would feel more noticeable on routes previously serviced by Spirit, the budget airline that shut down in May. Travelers are booking holiday flights earlier than they did in past years, a sign that prices are top of mind, she said.

Staying home for the holidays isn't a silver-bullet solution, either, as heating costs jump.

The average American household is expected to spend 7% more on home heating this winter than they did last year, according to the National Energy Assistance Directors Association. For the typical family, that equates to an increase of about $66 and a total bill for the season above $1,000.

To be sure, some families will feel it more than others. Heating oil, which is used mainly in homes in the Northeast, has jumped around 70% year over year. However, natural gas, which is one of the most common heating types in the U.S., costs about the same as last year.

Some U.S. states, like Massachusetts and New York, beefed up their home energy support programs to account for higher prices. But Mark Wolfe, NEADA's executive director, said federal aid offerings haven't been meaningfully expanded this year.

"Some of these increases are caused directly by the decision to go to war," Wolfe said. "Why isn't the federal government helping families pay these higher bills?"

The spirit of giving will take on a different meaning for consumers this year.

Retail sales during the all-important holiday season are expected to grow almost 5% this year and surpass $1 trillion for the first time, according to Bain & Company. But the consulting firm said most of that growth would come from inflation, rather than from consumers buying more products.

And Citi on Wednesday said that increased holiday spending would be mainly driven by higher earners this year. The bank said that's a result of the "K"-shaped economy, a term used to describe a scenario where better-off Americans splash out while others struggle.

Toy prices have climbed more than 9% over the past year and now sit at their highest levels since 2019, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics. Manufacturers have raised prices in response to higher costs for transportation and plastic, according to Chris Byrne, an independent toy industry analyst. The artificial intelligence boom has driven up prices for the chips that power gaming devices and other electronics.

Byrne said that's causing shoppers to focus more on the quality of gifts rather than quantity.

"The toy industry always reflects the culture at large," Byrne said. "It's almost true economically as well. As inflation is rising, people are being a little bit more cautious about what they're buying."

Just 16% of respondents to a Morning Consult survey said they planned to spend more in the 2026 holiday season than they did a year ago. Respondents who were older or had lower earnings were more likely to say their budgets were shrinking, the survey found.

Some are doing away with gifts altogether. A poll from consulting firm TSG and the Electronic Transactions Association found 5% of Americans did not plan to buy any presents in 2026, up three percentage points from last year.

One shopper, Wendy Cooper, set a limit of one gift for each of her three adult children this year. For her teenage daughter, the 56-year-old Atlanta resident has scoured Five Below, the trendy value retailer, for budget-friendly presents.

"It's super stressful," Cooper said. "You have to weigh: Do you guys want lights on and a place to live? Or do you want gifts?"

Across the board, the Bureau of Labor Statistics' consumer price index reaccelerated after the U.S. first struck Iran in late February. Annual inflation rates have begun rising faster than wages in the same period, leaving consumers with less purchasing power.

Even if Americans keep spending for the holidays, economists caution that high prices during this busy shopping period can further drag on their economic outlooks. In the CNBC Voter Poll early this month, about three-fourths of participants rated the economy as poor or not good. Consumer confidence gauges had dropped significantly this year, but have regained ground in the runup to the holiday shopping season.

From her home in Northern Michigan, Brecht is doing everything she can to make Christmas feel special without breaking the bank.

The graduate student has been delivering DoorDash orders in her spare time and listing clothes on resale platforms to save up money for her kids' holiday gifts. Brecht is watching closely for discounts and will look for potential presents at thrift stores in the coming weeks.

If necessary, Brecht said she would turn to credit cards for some holiday purchases. She isn't alone: TSG and ETA's survey found that about one-third of Americans said they expected to go into debt during the holiday season, a roughly 74% increase from 2025.

"I don't really know how we're going to do it," Brecht said. "I'm just praying for good Black Friday deals."

#2740. What happens if Lincoln Riley and USC lose to Madden Iamaleava?

The 2026 UCLA Bruin football season has been a success regardless of how things unfold on Saturday. Bob Chesney has helped the team match a single-season program win record and will have a few more chances to break that record.

Injuries have caught up to a UCLA team that didn't have an excess of talent that's required to overcome the obstacles of a full season. Chesney and the program will have a lot to hold their hats on even if the team falls in Week 13 to rival USC.

UCLA may or may not have quarterback Ty Dieffenbach available on Saturday as he works his way back from a hip injury. Dieffenbach has practiced this week but Madden Iamaleava may have to start for the Bruins, which would likely help USC's chances at the Rose Bowl.

There's a bit more pressure on the USC sideline. Lincoln Riley has added a lot of talent to the Trojans' program but that talent hasn't resulted in any College Football Playoff appearances for the program.

Trojans Wire writer Matt Zemek explained the pressure on Riley to get the win over UCLA if the team ends up facing Iamaleava.

"The only arguments for keeping Lincoln Riley if he loses to undermanned UCLA and Bob Chesney are the huge buyout and the reality that he will have another highly-rated recruiting class in 2027. "The cavalry is coming!" is and has been the USC mantra..  That's what the administration and athletic department think. I think Riley absolutely needs to be fired, because Alabama, Ohio State, Georgia, Texas, Oklahoma, LSU, and other big-boy programs wouldn't even give their coach a fourth season if the first three seasons didn't provide at least one playoff appearance."

"Most of those programs would probably fire a coach after two seasons if he didn't make the playoff. Here is USC giving Riley a sixth season after five swings and misses. It's ridiculous from my perspective, but USC will cite the buyout and the incoming recruiting class as reasons to keep Riley employed in 2027. It is really ironic that a UCLA win might be the only thing which could get Riley fired at this point. UCLA would make the playoff with a win, but it might give USC a chance to reboot. What would be absolutely spectacular for UCLA fans is if the Bruins and Chesney beat Riley and USC still retains Lincoln. USC fans would lose it, and I wouldn't blame them." Zemek wrote.

USC's chances at a CFP appearance in 2026 are nearly extinct, with USC likely to continue searching for their first playoff appearance in 2027. Saturday's game will still be hugely important to both teams but it won't overshadow the entire season for either program.

#2739. Packers Week 12 Opponent Preview: Who are this year’s Los Angeles Rams?

The Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams head into Wednesday's Week 12 matchup with matching 6-4 records, but they arrive headed in opposite directions. The Rams have won four straight, and their defense has allowed three touchdowns in five games, the fewest in the NFL. Green Bay lost 30-10 at home to Minnesota in their last game. Both teams are still trying to find some identity and could best be described as playoff bubble teams at this stage of the season.

The Packers have a chance to close the gap on Minnesota in the division with the Vikings' loss to San Francisco last Sunday night, whereas the Rams likely won't even catch their division rivals in that regard as the 49ers continue to widen the gap on them.

The Rams rebuilt their pass rush around Myles Garrett and a returning Aaron Donald and rank 2nd in defensive EPA/play. On offense they are missing Puka Nacua (out for the season) and Davante Adams (IR). Micah Parsons is cleared to make his 2026 debut for Green Bay.

Los Angeles went 12-5 and reached the NFC Championship Game, where they lost in a shootout to Seattle. The offense was the league's best by EPA/play (0.11, 1st), with 0.21 EPA per dropback and a 50.7% success rate. The defense ranked 11th in EPA/play allowed (-0.08). Then the Rams spent the offseason going all in on defense.

Edge Jared Verse was traded to Cleveland in the Myles Garrett deal.

OC Mike LaFleur left to become Arizona's head coach.

Assistant head coach Aubrey Pleasant was not retained.

Senior offensive assistant Alex Van Pelt left for Atlanta.

CB Cobie Durant and CB Derion Kendrick signed with Dallas.

OT Rob Havenstein and CB Darious Williams retired.

CB Roger McCreary, LB Troy Reeder, CB Ahkello Witherspoon, T D.J. Humphries and TE Nick Vannett also left.

TE Mark Redman was traded to Green Bay for a 2028 seventh-round pick.

Edge Myles Garrett was acquired from Cleveland for Verse, a 2027 first, a 2028 second and a conditional 2029 third.

CB Trent McDuffie was acquired from Kansas City for picks and signed a 4-year, $124M extension.

DT Aaron Donald came out of retirement on a 1-year, $20M deal.

CB Jaylen Watson signed for 3 years, $51M.

LB Grant Stuard signed for 2 years, $4.45M.

WR Tutu Atwell was reacquired from Miami.

QB Matthew Stafford re-signed for 1 year, $55M.

S Kamren Curl re-signed for 3 years, $36M.

TE Tyler Higbee re-signed for 2 years, $6M.

OC Nathan Scheelhaase was promoted from pass game coordinator.

Assistant head coach Kliff Kingsbury joined the staff.

Special teams coordinator Bubba Ventrone joined the staff.

QB Ty Simpson was a 1st-round pick (No. 13).

Chris Shula remains defensive coordinator, and Sean McVay still calls plays.

Touchdowns allowed, last five games: 3 (fewest in NFL)

W 34-6 at ARI: the line had its best game despite losing guard Steve Avila, center Coleman Shelton and backup Dylan McMahon. McVay used 20 personnel 17 times with a 59% success rate.

Protect Love against the best front he'll see this season. Garrett, Donald, Kobie Turner (back from a groin injury), Byron Young and Braden Fiske give the Rams five real pass rushers. Love throws about 36 times a game, and the Packers can't run the ball, so they have to win with quick throws and help for the tackles.

Keep taking deep shots. Love leads the NFL in 20-plus-yard completions, and Christian Watson (916 yards) and Matthew Golden (853 yards) have combined for over 1,700 yards. A secondary built around McDuffie and Watson will make those throws hard, but the big plays are Green Bay's offense.

Make Stafford beat you without his best receiver. With Nacua and Adams out, Higbee is the Rams' leading receiver with only 304 yards. Stafford has thrown 10 interceptions. Parsons has 3 sacks in two career games against Stafford, and Jonathan Gannon should test a patched-up offensive line.

Don't get out-schemed in the run game. The Rams rank about 6th in EPA per rush and succeed on 49% of plays, 3rd in the league. McVay is now leaning on two-back looks, so stopping the run on early downs keeps Stafford in obvious passing downs.

Win the margins. At 44, this is the lowest total of any Packers preview this season. Against a defense this good, field position, takeaways and red-zone touchdowns instead of field goals will decide it.

Injuries to watch: For Los Angeles, Puka Nacua and TE Terrance Ferguson are out for the season, Davante Adams is on IR, and guards Steve Avila and Coleman Shelton are out. For Green Bay, Micah Parsons is expected to make his season debut.

#2738. Aaron Rodgers Released By NFL’s Pittsburgh Steelers Following ESPN Interview Where He Criticized Coach Mike McCarthy After Benching

Aaron Rodgers has been released by the Pittsburgh Steelers following an explosive interview on ESPN's Pat McAfee Show.

The Super Bowl champion and 4x NFL MVP was benched this week, and backup quarterback Mason Rudolph was named to take his place in the Black Friday game against the Denver Broncos.

NFL On The Future Of Netflix's Thanksgiving Eve Game & Starting A New Tradition: "We Are Committed"

Rodgers, a frequent caller on the sports show, appeared to discuss the situation and, moments later, was confirmed to have been let go by the Steelers.

"I got the day off, so that's why we're able to do this," Rodgers said on the ESPN talk show.

Rodgers said he heard he would be inactive for Amazon's Prime Video game on Friday from a phone call with other members of the organization, not from Coach Mike McCarthy, whom he had spoken to earlier that day.

The football star noted that he had been talking to McCarthy in the last days, but talks about benching were not part of their conversation. Rodgers recalled his interactions with the Steelers coach in the last few days and claimed McCarthy came into a quarterback meeting "late, sat down and within 10 minutes began snoring."

Rodgers said that after waking himself up again, "five minutes later, you hear the snoring again and less than five minutes after that he walks out of the room like nothing happened." He added that McCarthy didn't show up to other meetings and walk-throughs that week.

The quarterback said he had expressed concern over McCarthy's health, approaching him as a friend, since sleeping during a meeting is a sign someone isn't healthy.

Later on in the interview, Rodgers suggested that what was best for the team was for McCarthy not to call the plays anymore, adding, "If he wasn't up for that, then they should probably release me."

"At this point, I'm not going back to Pittsburgh. That ship has definitely sailed," he said.

Shortly after his interview on The Pat McAfee Show, reports said Rodgers had been released.

Rodgers signed with the Steelers this season as a swan song and was planning to retire from the NFL at the end of the season. With Rodgers being released, any team can sign him until 4 p.m. ET on Thursday. If he goes unsigned, he would become a free agent.

#2737. What does life after Kevin Durant look like for the Rockets?

Alperen Sengun is 24. Amen Thompson and Jabari Smith Jr. are both 23. Tari Eason is 25. Kevin Durant is…

So is Fred VanVleet, but that feels inconsequential. Depending on where you stand, the Houston Rockets either need to replace him with a more dynamic offensive guard, solidify a succession plan that puts Reed Sheppard in his place, or replace him with a comparable low-turnover, game-managing point guard.

No matter where you land on VanVleet, replacing him is not a pressing imperative. It will be relatively easy.

Durant is averaging a career-low in field goal attempts per game by a considerable margin. He's shooting 13.4 shots per game, and his next-lowest career mark was 16.0 in 2022-23 with the Phoenix Suns.

That's either intentional or serendipitous.

For reference, that's effectively the same as, for example, the Minnesota Timberwolves' Jaden McDaniels (13.5). Let's use him as an example. Here's the existential question the Rockets need to be asking themselves:

If they replaced Durant with McDaniels, right now, how much worse would they be?

That's the type of player they can acquire relatively easily. Not McDaniels specifically: he's the premium model. He'll likely never be a Rocket, and he's just an example. More broadly, with their assets, the Rockets could find a high-end three-and-D wing tomorrow.

The offense might not be as different as you'd think. In 2026-27, Durant has a 65.4% True Shooting% (TS%) to McDaniels' 63.8%. The most devious devil in the details is that there's no massive gap between the two in shot creation this year. In 2026-27, 60.2% of Durant's two-point field goals have been assisted, and 86.7% of his threes, compared to 55.7% on twos and 93.1% on threes for McDaniels.

The Rockets don't seem to need Durant to create offense at a high volume. The Rockets' 121.1 Offensive Rating is the best in the NBA. Surprisingly enough, defense has been an issue this year, as their 113.0 Defensive Rating ranks just 11th. Wait a minute:

Durant has been the best (and most expensive) decoy in the NBA in 2026-27. His gravity is important to Alperen Sengun's functionality as a hub, especially when so many of the team's actions revolve around Amen Thompson cutting while he's in the high post. That partly works because there's a guy on the perimeter that the defense absolutely cannot leave.

So, let's assume the Rockets don't need Durant's creation, but still need his gravity. This gets tougher. Defenses won't guard McDaniels the way they guard Durant. They would scramble to make sure that, say, Corey Kispert isn't open, but he can't defend well enough to find regular minutes anywhere.

We know the Rockets will lean defense. Suppose they draft the next McDaniels in 2027, or sign someone, or whatever: Imagine they bring in a high-end three-and-D who doesn't create much offense to replace Durant when he retires.

They will take a step back, but I propose that it won't be too substantial a step. I can live with that:

Fundamentally, that's what this boils down to. If the Rockets can stomach a quick fix, replacing Durant with a young(er), low-usage, high-impact wing should suffice. If not, they'll need to be star-hunting as Durant approaches the end of his career. By that logic, he'll need to be replaced both positionally and functionally, so they could get that low-usage wing and a star guard, or vice versa.

Only then can this room be elephant-free.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2736. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

For more reaction to Wednesday's rebound and specific trading ideas, join host Oliver Renick, CNBC contributor Mike Khouw, and guest Scott Bauer on Options Action LIVE from Cboe, by clicking the video above.With stocks mounting a comeback Wednesday, options trading across a few key volatility products suggests big swings in stocks may dampen in the near term.

The fund aims to track the moves in Cboe's VIX Index, which surged to 35 early Wednesday but slipped to below 28 as the S&P 500 rallied to within 25 basis points of unchanged.

At the same time, options volume on VIX itself was 50% above average, with an even more distinctive bias towards puts, suggesting traders see stocks firming, or at least, the size of the market's moves shrinking. Of the 850,000 options traded on VIX, more than 515,000 were puts, with twice as many puts likely initiated by buyers than calls, Cboe data show.

#2733. Daniel Dubois vs Joseph Parker set to deliver ‘blockbuster beginning to 2027’

The heavyweight division continues to be involved in many boxing conversations, with Tyson Fury vs Anthony Joshua edging closer. In addition, the current WBO champion Daniel Dubois is arguably the most in-form in the division after his two big wins over Fabio Wardley. Queensberry Promotions have now announced Daniel Dubois vs Joseph Parker.

Daniel Dubois vs Joseph Parker (36-4, 24 KOs) was originally slated for February 2025, when Dubois, the then IBF champion, had to withdraw in fight week due to illness. Parker then stepped up to face late replacement Martin Bakole, whom he stopped in the second round.

With that win over Bakole, Parker retained his WBO interim title. The Kiwi-Samoan then faced Fabio Wardley and was stopped in dramatic fashion in the eleventh round. Parker was then hit by a huge double blow after the fight; firstly, it was revealed he had failed a UKAD test for cocaine, which was later ruled Parker's nutritionist was the reason that traces were found. Secondly, Wardley was upgraded to the full champion after Usyk vacated his WBO belt.

The 34-year-old should be applauded for taking this opportunity for his first fight back.

Dubois (24-3, 23 KOs) has recorded brilliant wins against Wardley x2 and Anthony Joshua among others. He fell short against Oleksandr Usyk on two occasions, and lost to Joe Joyce earlier in his career, but Dubois has improved immensely as he gets older. His wins over Jarrell Miller and Filip Hrgovic were also inspired. He has shown a willingness to face the best, and that has to be congratulated. His jab is like a power punch, and his right hand carries huge power.

This is a huge fight for both, for very different reasons.

Daniel Dubois will defend his WBO title against Joseph Parker on Saturday, January 30, 2027, at Co-op Live, Manchester, live on DAZN.

Parker won the WBO title in 2016 and has a strong resume, recording wins against Deontay Wilder, Zhilei Zhang, Martin Bakole and Derek Chisora twice.

Setting the scene for this heavyweight dust-up, Queensberry founder Frank Warren said:

"This heavyweight showdown will deliver a blockbuster beginning to 2027. It's a fight everyone wanted to see, and I'm thrilled we can now make it happen. Daniel is entering his prime and when he is on his game it is hard to picture anybody beating him. But Joseph has long promised to become world champion again, and now he gets his chance. 2027 will be a year for the heavyweight history books, and this is the first of many world title fights to be staged by Queensberry and DAZN next year."

This is an intriguing matchup, and a marker laid down by Queensberry to their promotional rivals.

Picture By Mark Robinson Matchroom Boxing.

As we mentioned earlier, Dubois' jab is like a power punch. When he uses it, it enables him to boss the tempo in his fights and set up for the knockout punch over the top. From the first bell against Joshua, he used that jab to disrupt AJ and set the tempo, and he never let his fellow Brit back in before delivering the KO shot. He throws everything with spiteful intent. The two wins over Wardley showed just how much he's improved under the tutelage of Don Charles.

Parker is a smart fighter and a sharp counterpuncher. He will look to make Dubois miss and punish him. He picks his spots well, and stays calm under pressure. His own jab is a vital tool of his, and he will need to establish it quickly and not let Dubois get in a groove. Parker has quick hands and puts his combinations together well.

He will pose questions to Dubois, but we believe "Dynamite" will have the answers, and the pressure and power from the Brit will prove the difference, and Dubois will win via a late stoppage.

#2732. Saudi national in OpenAI bugging case is out on bond after DOJ tries to block release

Abdulrahman Alfozan, the Saudi national accused of bugging an executive conference room at OpenAI's headquarters, has been released from custody, his attorney told CNBC on Wednesday.

The confirmation of Alfozan's release came a day after a federal judge in San Francisco said the defendant would be allowed out of jail on a $1 million bond, despite prosecutors' warnings that he posed a serious flight risk.

Alfozan, a former OpenAI engineer, is expected to be fitted with an ankle monitor, his attorney, David Rizk, previously told CNBC.

Alfozan was scheduled to be released from detention Wednesday morning at 8 a.m. PT. Shortly before that deadline, U.S. Attorney Craig Missakian of the Northern District of California urged the court to maintain Alfozan's detention pending trial, "given the significant risk that the defendant will flee to Saudi Arabia if he is released from custody."

In an order posted on the case docket later Wednesday, Judge Vince Chhabria said the court only learned of the government's last-minute bid to block Alfozan's release after he had already been let out.The government's motion "is therefore denied as moot," Chhabria's order stated. The judge set a hearing for Dec. 3 on the bid to revoke the magistrate judge's order releasing Alfozan.

Pointing to Alfozan's "ample resources, significant family and professional ties to Saudi Arabia, and few remaining ties to the United States," Missakian had argued in a court filing that the release conditions imposed by Magistrate Judge Alex Tse "are insufficient to safeguard the defendant's appearance for future proceedings in this case."

Alfozan "presents a serious risk of flight that no set of release conditions can reasonably mitigate," Missakian wrote.

His office sought to pause the magistrate judge's release order until a ruling on a motion to revoke it. The Department of Justice did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment on Alfozan's release.

Tse in a court hearing Tuesday ruled in favor of Rizk's motion for his client's release, which said prosecutors have admitted finding "no new evidence to support the theory that Mr. Alfozan was stealing trade secrets, or to support the speculation that he was acting as an unregistered foreign agent for Saudi Arabia."

In a text message to CNBC after that hearing, Rizk said, "We are simply grateful that the Court made the right decision to release Mr. Alfozan and now we look forward to defending the case."

But Missakian's filing Wednesday morning pushed back, saying "the government's investigation of this seven-week-old case is ongoing."

Prosecutors have so far found "hundreds of audio files" on Alfozan's personal computer, "including dozens of recordings of meetings or conversations that occurred at OpenAI throughout September 2026, at least one of which involved representatives of a foreign government and many of which involved discussions of what may be trade secrets," according to the filing.

"To be sure, what the defendant did or intended to do with those audio files remains under investigation, but the absence of that evidence at this point has no bearing" on whether Alfozan is a flight risk, Missakian argued.

Alfozan was arrested Oct. 9 and is accused of concealing a recording device in the artificial intelligence company's conference room to secretly record discussions. The device was found in a USB port in a television in the room on Sept. 30, prosecutors have said.

Jury selection for Alfozan's trial is scheduled to begin Jan. 20.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan contributed to this report.

#2729. Florida vs Florida State Prediction: Last Sunshine State Showdown Before a New Era

There was a time when Florida vs Florida State was one of the biggest games of the college football season. It might get there again soon, but for now, the rivalry is in a holding pattern.Florida is 20th in the latest College Football Playoff rankings after winning two straight to turn the season back around after a rough stretch. Florida State is waiting for new head coach Glenn Schumann to start, but the Georgia defensive coordinator is still a little busy for a bit at his current gig. He's on the way, but for now, the Seminoles can close the season strong with a win over the Gators and become bowl eligible.

Nov 1, 2025; Jacksonville, Florida, USA; Georgia Bulldogs defensive coordinator Glenn Schumann watches from the sideline against the Florida Gators at EverBank Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Travis Register-Imagn Images

Florida started the season 4-0, lost four of its next five games, and then beat Kentucky and Vanderbilt. Win this, and it'll be the program's first eight-win regular season since 2020, when the Gators played for the SEC title.

Florida State has played well under interim head coach Rick Stockstill, but when it had a chance for a sixth win and bowl eligibility, it lost to NC State 28-10 last week. It gets one more shot.

Florida has won five of the last seven in the series.

Date: Friday, November 27, 2026Game Time: 3:30 PM ETTV: ABCLocation: Doak Campbell Stadium, Tallahassee, FL – Week 13 Schedule, Game Previews– CFP and Bowl Projections

Florida State has to run well to win.The passing game is dangerous, but the Noles don't win when they bomb away. They win when they get things going on the ground, going 4-1 when running for 175 yards or more. It's possible to run on the Gators, but Florida State isn't Georgia, or even Missouri, when it comes to being able to grind.And then there's the other side. Florida is 7-0 when running for more than 120 yards. Run well on the Noles, beat the Noles.

Everyone wants to win a rivalry game like this, and of course Florida will bring the full focus, but this is existential for the Seminoles. It'll be iffy whether or not the program would want to accept a bowl invite, or be able to, considering the regime change, but this is for six wins. This is for a bowl game.Florida's defense isn't bad, but it doesn't come up with enough third down stops. Florida State has to control the positive momentum from the start. It doesn't need big plays, but with its tempo, it has to keep the Gators on their heels with a good offensive balance.

This should be fun. There's a new energy around the Florida State program, the team will come up with a strong first quarter, and then the Gator offense will start to take over.It might not start working right away, but Florida's offense will keep steadily producing. The flash might not be there, but it won't need to be.

#2728. Fed’s preferred inflation gauge accelerated to 3.8% in October, raising the 2027 stakes for the central bank

Inflation moved further away from the Federal Reserve's target in October, reinforcing expectations that another interest rate hike is in the cards for December and raising questions about how much more work the central bank will have to do in 2027.

The personal consumption expenditures price index, the Fed's preferred inflation gauge, showed a seasonally adjusted 3.8% annual inflation rate, in line with the Dow Jones consensus estimate and nearly twice the central bank's goal. The reading was 0.1 percentage point higher than September.

Excluding food and energy, core PCE was 3.3%, the Commerce Department reported Wednesday. That was 0.1 percentage point above the forecast.

While the Fed officially follows the headline figure, policymakers consider core to be a more reliable longer-term gauge of inflation trends.

A separate report also showed much stronger economic growth for the third quarter, with gross domestic product rising at a 4.3% annualized rate, according to a revised Commerce Department estimate. The strong top-line gain for the economy adds further fuel to the notion that Fed monetary policy is not restricting economic activity.

Consumer spending rose 0.3% for the month, which matched the pace of price increases but was 0.1 percentage point lower than forecast. Income, on the other hand, surged 0.7%, much more than the 0.4% estimate. The personal saving rate rose to 4.1%.

Stock market futures added to losses following the release, while Treasury yields also were sharply lower. Even with the higher-than-expected core inflation figure, expectations eased a bit for Fed rate hikes in 2027. Traders continued to price in two more increases but lowered odds for a third hike to about 1-in-3, compared to 50-50 on Tuesday, according to the CME Group's FedWatch.

"Inflation is too hot and it will not be brought under control as long as economic demand is also too hot," said Chris Rupkey, chief economist at Fwdbonds.

The report comes with the market fully pricing in a quarter percentage point interest rate hike when the Fed meets Dec. 8-9. Less certain is how much more rates will need to increase to get inflation back down to the Fed's 2% target. Inflation has been running above target for some 5½ years.

Inflation had been drifting lower but spiked again following the late-February attacks on Iran led by the U.S. and Israel. Tariffs also have continued to exert pressure, as has strong demand associated with the artificial intelligence buildout. The White House on Wednesday announced a fresh round of tariffs on 14 trading partners including China, the European Union and Japan.

Policymakers generally consider factors such as tariffs and supply shocks to be temporary, but some fear the impact has begun spreading through the economy and could become embedded.

"America's inflation problem is bigger than an energy shock," said Heather Long, chief economist at Navy Federal Credit Union.

The large upward increase in GDP came from revisions to investment as well as consumer and government spending.

Consumer spending, which accounts for about two-thirds of all economic activity, rose 4.2% in the third quarter. A metric known as real final sales to private domestic purchasers, which Fed officials regard as a key indicator of underlying demand, jumped 5.8%, reflecting an upward revision of 0.9 percentage point.

In other economic news Wednesday, the Labor Department reported that initial jobless claims slipped to 189,000 for the week ending Nov. 21, down 13,000 from the prior week and back to around the lowest level since the late 1960s. Wall Street had been expecting 200,000.

#2727. 2026-27 NBA Rookies Report Card 2.0, Grades Nov. 25: Two players get an A: Cameron Boozer, Yaxel Landeborg

No one is going to confuse me with a Harvard professor, but it feels like "grade inflation" has hit Rookie Report Cards 2.0. Turns out, this highly touted draft class is largely living up to its billing, with many of these players not just showing promise (if not efficiency yet), but also contributing to winning. There are a lot of good grades, and anything below a B feels a bit harsh this early in the season (and their careers). With that intro, here's our second round of Rookie Report Cards.

2026-27 Stats: 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 41.7% shooting

What Boozer is doing with Memphis is what he has done at every stop on his basketball journey: The stats are impressive, but they don't begin to tell the whole story. Boozer simply contributes to winning, doing whatever the team needs on a given night, and he has been completely at ease as the face of the franchise since he walked in the door. He has been everything the Grizzlies hoped for when they drafted him.

Boozer has been a big part of Memphis' fast start, as has their depth — and that depth will be put to the test for the next month with Boozer out due to a hamstring strain. Injuries suck.

2026-27 Stats: 19.2 points, 7.4 rebounds, 3.6 assists, 45.2% shooting

Heading into last June's draft, the league-wide consensus was that the top four picks were in a class of their own, and everyone else was a tier below. Lendeborg has blown that logic up early this season and should be considered a frontrunner for Rookie of the Year. He ranks near the top of this rookie class in pretty much every statistical category — points (second), rebounds (second), blocks (fourth) — and his lowest is sixth in assists. He's shooting 36.7% from 3-point range and has been incredibly efficient for a rookie.

Things have been tougher for Lendeborg since Stephen Curry went out and he became the guy at the top of opponent scouting reports — asking him to lead the offense and be the focus of the defense is a lot for a rookie. That said, Lendeborg has still played well and is exactly the guy the Warriors needed.

2026-27 Stats: 7.9 points, 2.7 rebounds, 4.2 assists, 48.8% shooting

It took just nine games for Flemmings to replace veteran CJ McCollum in the starting lineup in Atlanta, a sign of how good he's looked to start the season — and how good he's looked in practices to coach Quin Snyder and the staff. He has just returned from missing a few games with a back issue.

He is a two-way player who brings physicality and defends well. In Summer League, his decision-making off the pick-and-roll showed promise — and he has carried that over to the regular season, especially since moving into the starting lineup. What was unimpressive at Summer League was his shooting, but he has found his form to start the season, and while the volume is low, he is knocking down 40% of his 3s so far this season. Atlanta may just have the point guard they were seeking.

2026-27 Stats: 11.4 points, 5.7 rebounds, 2.5 assists, 45.5% shooting

I am higher on Burries play so far than many others, and this early-season grade might be optimistic, but I like what I see. Maybe nobody is noticing because few people are watching Milwaukee right now, but once Taylor Jenkins moved Burries back to the bench a couple of games ago, it seemed to unlock something, he's playing much more free and much more efficiently. On the season, he has a below-average 52.4 true shooting percentage as a starter, but that jumps to an impressive 63 as a reserve.

There is legitimate concern about his 3-point shot (now up to 31.3% for the season), but just watching him play, Burries is a reason for hope for a Bucks fanbase that needs something to get behind at the start of a rebuilding process.

2026-27 Stats: 12.7 points, 2.7 rebounds, 1.8 assists, 47.2% shooting

Acuff's running mate at Arkansas, who fell to the No. 34 pick, looks like the steal of the second round. This grade is based partly on the expectations vs. production the Cavaliers have gotten from him — he's started three games and is getting 18+ minutes a night, something impressive for any rookie.

Thomas is a shooter, knocking down 40.7% on 3-pointers. We should have seen this coming when he led Summer League in scoring, averaging 27.3 points per game. He's got room to grow — he isn't much of a playmaker yet and isn't driving and drawing a lot of fouls — but he is knocking down shots in a league where that is a highly prized skill.

2026-27 Stats: 16.5 points, 6.9 rebounds, 2.8 assists, 42.2% shooting

It's a little hard to grade Dybantsa because it feels like he's being limited by his environment. This man is a strong, physical driver who has to wait his turn as the third guy in the pecking order behind Anthony Davis (great start to the season) and Trae Young (um... not as great a start to the season, let's say). Plus, Alex Sarr is a quality young big, but he's not spacing the floor or creating driving lanes. Still, we see Dybantsa's undeniable talent shine through at moments.

Dybantsa seems to be breaking through of late, with three double-doubles in his last four games and 19.8 points and 10.3 rebounds in those contests. The Wizards need to build on that and feature Dybantsa more and let him grow and learn through some mistakes. His grade is this low only because a left ankle sprain delayed his start to the season and, with that, his development is maybe a little behind where it could be, but he's starting to really demand more time on the ball and more opportunities.

2026-27 Stats: 15.9 points, 4.5 rebounds, 4 assists, 40.1% shooting

Kind of like Dybantsa in Washington, the challenge with Peterson in Utah has been fit. You can try to label Peterson and Utah's other young guard, Keyonte George, as point and shooting guards (in whatever order you wish), the reality is they are both scoring guards who want to attack. Then, take Lauri Markkanen and Jaren Jackson Jr. out of the picture in Utah due to injuries and it's just hard to get a read on Peterson at times.

That said, Peterson is improving — he had a stretch of five 20+ point games a few weeks ago — and more importantly, he's getting to the rim more and not settling for midrange jumpers as he did earlier in the season. He is at his best when he is an aggressive, downhill attacker reading the defense, and the Jazz need to encourage more of that and less of him pulling up or taking floaters. Let him be himself, working through the growing pains.

2026-27 Stats: 24.6 points, 3.4 rebounds, 7.2 assists, 40.3% shooting

While some high draft picks are having to stand in line to get touches and opportunities, that is not the case with Acuff — he is at the front of the line for the all-you-can-eat buffet of shots in Sacramento. He's third in the league in shot attempts per game. He actually leads the NBA in average time of possession — he has the ball for 7.2 minutes per game.

The Kings' record this season is what you get when you turn the keys to the offense over to a 6'2" rookie guard, and no doubt his efficiency and ability to lift up teammates must get better. What you have to like is his relentlessness, and he shows he knows how to read the pick-and-roll. There is a lot to build on, but Acuff also has a long way to go. As do the Kings.

2026-27 Stats: 10 points, 7.1 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 64.9% shooting

Thrust into a larger role with Chet Holmgren out, what you have to like about Mara is that he plays within himself — he knows what he does well, where he can impact games, and he stays in that box. He's not going to space the floor (which is why the double-big lineups with him and Isaiah Hartenstein can have issues). What he can do is protect the paint, set screens and score around the rim. His defense has been fantastic, and the Thunder have needed that as they battle through a rash of injuries.

Caleb Wilson, Grade: Incomplete (B- in games he played)

2026-27 Stats: 18.9 points, 6.2 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 42% shooting

He's only played in 10 games due to a fractured hand, which is a setback in his development and the reason for the incomplete grade (something that will change later in the season). It's also a freak injury beyond his control, a fluke.

If we base the grade on the 10 games he played, you see a player with immense talent — maybe the highest ceiling of any player in this class — who has already put together an impressive highlight reel.nBut he is so raw and making so many rookie mistakes — turnovers, missed 3s that he should pass on — it's hard to grade him. Do we grade him on the potential? Then it's an "A," no question. However, if we're judging him as a rookie player, as I have done, the grade drops, maybe B- is too harsh, but it's where I landed. For now. I expect that by the end of the season his grade will be much higher. He will figure it out.

2026-27: 11.2 points, 3.5 rebounds, 2.9 assists, 34.4% shooting

Part of the challenge in grading Wagler is that he comes off the bench at a stacked position for the team. Wagler is in the same space as veterans Derrick Jones Jr., Kris Dunn and Max Strus, and Tyronn Lue needs to showcase those veterans for potential trades. Lue has admitted he needs to find Wagler more minutes.

There are a lot of things Wagler does well, he can knock down 3s (he's been impressive as a shooter of late after a slow start), and he knows how to get to the free throw line. The concern is the 34% shooting on two-pointers, a lot of them from floater range. All of that should improve, and his game will come around if he gets enough minutes on the court.

2026-27: 6.3 points, 4.7 rebounds, 1.4 assists, 42.2% shooting

This grade may be a little harsh for Johnson, who had injuries slowing him in training camp and is just 20 years old (Johnson is more on Cooper Flagg's timeline than the 24-year-old Lendeborg). That said, he has struggled with fouls on the defensive end, and is not converting enough of his offensive opportunities. There are flashes, but he has a long way to go to be the long-term forward pairing with Cooper Flagg that the Mavericks drafted him to be. It's early in the season and his career, but Johnson has a lot of work to do.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

Contracts ask traders if the S&P 500 will go above various levels in 2026, and they're resolved using data from S&P Dow Jones Indices.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The S&P 500 has slashed its year-to-date gain to just over 2%.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2723. U.S. proposes new tariffs on 14 trading partners, including China and the EU

The U.S. is preparing new tariffs on 14 economies, including China, the EU, Japan and India, following investigations into what the Trump administration says are excess manufacturing practices that disadvantage U.S. commerce.

The administration launched those and other trade investigations in March, weeks after the Supreme Court unraveled a major piece of President Donald Trump's tariff-heavy economic policy by striking down his "liberation day" duties. Trump has since been accused by critics of his tariffs of trying to revive the canceled duties using other legal authorities.

The latest action targets foreign economies that create, sustain or contribute to structural excess capacity and production in manufacturing sectors that the U.S. finds unreasonable and burdensome or restrictive to its commerce, a senior U.S. official told reporters Tuesday evening.

The new tariffs are "targeted," not blanket rates, and will vary for each economy, and many will not exceed the rates established in previously reached trade deals, said the official, who spoke on condition of anonymity to discuss the forthcoming duties.

The rates, which mostly range from 15% to 20%, are "not really additive" and generally will not stack on top of existing duties, U.S. Trade Representative Jamieson Greer told CNBC's "Squawk Box" Wednesday morning.

"In effect, it'll look more like a top up," Greer said. "When it all nets out, it really will just top up a few percentage points."

An exception is China, which faces a 20% rate under the new tariff policy, "so it will go up some," Greer said. But at the same time, both countries are moving to remove tariffs on some of each other's goods in what Greer called "stability-generating activity through this action."

The senior U.S. official said Tuesday that the 20% rate will come on top of the so-called forced technology transfer-related tariffs of roughly 25%, which were implemented during Trump's first presidential term. U.S. tariffs on Chinese goods will therefore reach about 45%, give or take for different goods, the official said.

Average U.S. tariffs rates on Chinese goods stood at about 36.5% as of July, according to the Congressional Research Service.

Greer also said on CNBC that the new policy likely will not take effect for months.

"We've talked to our trading partners about this. They know what we're proposing," he said. "I think a lot of this is baked in, and I don't think any of these would actually take effect earlier than a couple months from now."

The administration says the tariffs target what it calls "overcapacity" — excess industrial production, often supported by government subsidies, that allows cheaper goods to flood global markets and undercut U.S. producers.

China has disputed the allegations. "The so-called 'overcapacity' does not really exist," China's foreign ministry said Wednesday before the official reports were released by the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative, according to Reuters.

The overcapacity levies are being imposed under Section 301 of the Trade Act of 1974. The Trump administration invoked the same statute to launch investigations into 60 economies over alleged forced-labor issues.

Those investigations, which also began in March, led to broad tariffs of 10% to 12.5% on goods from dozens of countries covering 99.4% of U.S. imports.

The Section 301 actions came weeks after the Supreme Court struck down the near-global tariffs that Trump had enacted under the International Emergency Economic Powers Act.

Shortly after the court's ruling, Trump invoked Section 122 of the Trade Act of 1974 to impose a 10% global tariff. That tariff expired after 150 days, and was immediately replaced with the forced-labor-related duties.

An ongoing lawsuit accuses the Trump administration of citing forced-labor issues as a "pretext" in pursuit of reviving Trump's worldwide tariff regime.

#2722. SpaceX wins FCC approval to deploy 1 million orbital data center satellites

The Federal Communications Commission has authorized Elon Musk's SpaceX to put 1 million artificial intelligence computing satellites in orbit, Chairman Brendan Carr announced on Wednesday.

Carr said in a post on X, the social network owned by SpaceX, that the company's Starmind constellation, and "competitor constellations will use solar power to extend America's lead" in AI.

Musk said in a reply, "Much appreciated. This will be a great asset to America and the world."

The FCC, in its authorization filing, noted that SpaceX's application "received a substantial number of comments from the public, including petitions to deny filed by Kuiper Systems LLC (Amazon Leo), Viasat Inc. (Viasat), and the American Astronomical Society (AAS)."

In a March petition urging the FCC to deny SpaceX's application, Amazon had labeled the company's Starmind project as a "lofty ambition rather than a real plan."

The e-commerce and cloud computing giant is building a competitor to SpaceX's Starlink satellite internet service, and said it would take SpaceX "centuries" to build the constellation, adding that the company provided scant details about how it would "deliver on these grand claims."

The petition resulted in a rebuke from Carr, who said Amazon "should focus on the fact" that it's falling short of its own satellite deployment milestone.

Amazon declined to comment on the FCC's approval.

Other companies have also set their sights on orbital data centers. Google last year announced Project Suncatcher, which it describes as a "moonshot to put AI in space," and launched its first chips into space on a SpaceX rocket and Planet Labs satellite in October.

In March, Jeff Bezos' Blue Origin petitioned the FCC to let it launch up to 51,600 satellites as part of a low Earth orbit satellite constellation dubbed "Project Sunrise." The constellation would work in tandem with Blue Origin's planned TeraWave satellite internet service, announced in January.

Astrophysicist and author Adam Becker was critical of the FCC move in an email to CNBC on Wednesday.

"Any serious attempt at this is going to further pollute the night sky and runs a serious risk of a cascading chain reaction of space junk collisions, which would be a disaster for all of humanity," he wrote. "Musk and Carr are either profoundly stupid themselves, or hoping the rest of us are too stupid to notice this is nonsense."

SpaceX did not respond to a request for comment.

The FCC said in its authorization that it dismissed "comments and concerns raised regarding topics over which the Commission has no authority, including environmental concerns," and concerns about its impact on "optical astronomy."

#2720. The Nasdaq is in freefall. Here's one stock to look at

I asked Claude after Friday's close what I should do about the tanking stock market. Its advice was surprisingly useful: avoid panic selling and look for opportunities. A 40 VIX, which it touched Friday, might provide those opportunities. Specifically, a higher VIX (higher options premiums) signals fear, but it also creates an opportunity to sell elevated premium in some premium stocks, like Amazon.

Amazon's cloud business is growing at its fastest pace in nearly five years. AWS revenue rose 39.3% YoY in the third quarter, and management reported a $578 billion backlog. Trailing 12-month operating cash flow rose 29% to $168.3 billion. However, infrastructure investment accelerated, resulting in more than $42 billion in negative cash flow. The competing forces of accelerating growth and the cost of supplying it could keep the stock in its range over the next four weeks. Its shares are only off 14% from its recent highs, comparatively better than the Nasdaq Composite's near 19% decline.

AWS demand supports the lower end of the range. The backlog gives Amazon a basis for adding capacity even as investors scrutinize the cash cost. Cash spending could temper an upside rerating.

The next leg higher may require evidence that new capacity can earn attractive returns at this scale, and that evidence may be in short supply over the next couple of months.

Sell the Dec. 18, 2026 AMZN 235/285 strangle for an indicative $5.90 credit per share.

Max reward — $590 per strangle if AMZN finishes between $235 and $285 at expiration, before fees.

Breakevens at expiration — $229.10 and $290.90.

Max risk — Unlimited on the uncovered call, but one should think of the trade as risking getting short at a net price above $290, a nearly 13% premium to today's closing price. The downside loss would reach $22,910 per strangle if AMZN fell to zero. The credit is roughly 2.3% of a $260 reference share price over four weeks, or nearly 30% on a simple annualized basis. That comparison uses the share price as its denominator. Actual return on capital depends on margin and the cost to close or settle the trade.

Buying in bear markets, as the Nasdaq just touched, is never easy. But those opportunities don't present themselves too often. What are you going to do with it?

#2719. Sixers look to get back on track as they resume NBA cup play vs. Miami

It appears the Sixers' next test without Joel Embiid will be a challenging one. Philadelphia hosts the Miami Heat the Wednesday before Thanksgiving, tipping off at 5 p.m. to start an entire evening of NBA Cup action. Miami leads Eastern Conference Group B at 2-0 coming into this one. The Sixers are in third place at 1-1 and have a minus-19 point differential.

The Heat have gotten out to a lead in the group while riding a seven-game winning streak entering play today. After a slow start to the season, their average margin of victory has been 11.2 points over those seven games. Like the Sixers, this new Heat team hasn't looked quite as expected to start the year. They're 6th in points scored per 100 possessions but 17th in points allowed per CleaningTheGlass.

They come to town at a bad time for the Sixers, likely to be without Joel Embiid for the second straight game. Embiid is listed as doubtful with an illness. For Miami, their big acquisition Giannis Antetekounmpo is questionable with groin tendinitis. Antetekounmpo did miss their last game against the Oklahoma City Thunder, but seems set to return.

The pairing of Antetekounmpo and Bam Adebayo projects to give an undersized Sixers' frontcourt plenty of problems. In the six games Antetekounmpo has played during this winning streak, he's averaged 29.5 points per game with a 68.4% effective field goal percentage.

Dominick Barlow is probably the best option to guard Giannis, but the Sixers have yet to give him a heavy workload since returning from his back injury, and for good reason. After playing 11 minutes in his debut, Barlow did get up to 16 in his second game back, but was favoring his back after taking a couple of spills.

The Heat have been hot from outside as of late, shooting 39% from three or better in each of their las three games. That could be a problem for a Sixers team that has not been sharp defending the point of attack as of late. The good news is after shipping out some of their younger wings, namely Jaime Jacquez Jr., this Miami team isn't the offensive rebounding machine they have been in years past. They rank 21st so far this season in offensive rebounding percentage.

The key for the Sixers might be a big game for Tyrese Maxey, who's been quiet for his standards offensively. In his last three games he's averaged 21 points per game.

Jaylen Brown did look fantastic in the first half of his first game back. He shot 8-of-10 from the floor for 19 points, easily getting to whatever spot on the floor he wanted. When he picked up his fourth foul he had to sit for nearly the entire third quarter and that greatly disrupted his rhythm. Brown didn't make a field goal in the second half and admitted after the game he felt physically different.

Getting more of that first half Brown would greatly help the Sixers' chances against Miami, but more importantly, Sixers fans should hope that isn't a sign of Brown's hamstring injury lingering.

#2718. Vance says he was assured Jensen Huang was not under DOJ investigation

Vice President JD Vance said Wednesday that Attorney General Todd Blanche told him Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang was not under investigation.

"I was told by the Department of Justice and multiple officials in our national security apparatus that he was under no investigation," Vance told reporters in Ohio on Wednesday after being asked what assurances he had gotten that Huang was not under investigation. "Not even that he was about to be prosecuted; that he's under no investigation."

Vance spoke two days after President Donald Trump told CNBC in an interview that an investigation had been done.

Trump was asked what Blanche had told him about potential criminal liability for Huang and the question of whether Nvidia chips had ended up in China in violation of U.S. export laws.

"They did a pretty good investigation, and they felt that he, himself, was exonerated," Trump said Monday.

Reuters reported Wednesday that the Department of Justice did not conduct a formal investigation into Huang before Trump pushed for his reinstatement as CEO.

Blanche had concluded there was no evidence Huang had broken any laws based on investigations into Nvidia clients Super Micro Computer and Megaspeed, according to the report.

The DOJ did not immediately respond to a request for comment on the Reuters report. And the White House didn't immediately respond to a request for comment on Vance saying Huang was under no investigation when Trump had said there was one. Nvidia didn't immediately respond to a request for comment about Vance's remarks.

Blanche on Tuesday dodged questions about whether there had been an investigation.

"What the president said is that he talked to a lot of people going into the decision that he made to encourage the board to reinstate him," Blanche said after being asked whether the DOJ had investigated Huang and, if so, what were the results of the probe.

Huang was reinstated as CEO of Nvidia on Monday, after the chipmaker's board of directors said it had received "input from senior levels of the United States government" that Huang was not a national security risk. Trump took credit for Huang's reinstatement in the interview with CNBC shortly after Nvidia's announcement.

Huang resigned following The New York Times reporting multiple affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who is alleged to be a spy for Beijing and who was an executive for the Singapore-based cloud provider Megaspeed.

The reporting added fuel to allegations that Nvidia should have known its artificial intelligence chips were being diverted to China and being used by Chinese companies, violating U.S. export laws.

"The product they create is a very important part of us staying ahead of the SI arms race with China. We really do care about ensuring those chips do not fall into the wrong hands," Vance said, using the Trump administration's preferred acronym for AI, which the president calls "super intelligence."

"We actually see this is an opportunity for Nvidia to focus on complying with American export controls, and we think Jensen Huang is going to take that very seriously," Vance continued.

"We received the assurances that he was not under any significant — he was under no investigation — and the accusations against him were based on anonymous reporting, not on actual evidence," Vance said.

On Tuesday, Trump left the door open to pardon Huang in an interview with the New York Post, but Vance said on Wednesday that the CEO doesn't need one.

"I don't think he needs a pardon at all," Vance said. "I haven't seen any evidence again that he committed a crime. And if there is evidence that he committed a crime, then that's up to relevant authorities to actually bring a prosecution against him."

Huang is scheduled to appear for a closed-door interview with House committees about his dealings with Megaspeed on Tuesday. At the same time, Nvidia will hold its annual conference where it showcases new technology in Washington.

— Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.

#2717. Atlanta boosts left-handed pitching depth with latest signing

Atlanta boosts left-handed pitching depth with latest signing appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Atlanta Braves are continuing to add options to their pitching staff as they prepare for the 2027 season. Their latest move brings another left-handed arm into the organization, giving Atlanta additional depth ahead of Spring Training.

According to New York Post reporter Jon Heyman, Atlanta has agreed to a minor-league contract signing left-hander Charlie Barnes. The 31-year-old will also receive an invitation to Major League Spring Training. Barnes has previously appeared in the majors with the Minnesota Twins, Chicago Cubs, and Los Angeles Dodgers.

Barnes made five MLB appearances during the 2026 campaign, including one with Chicago and four with Los Angeles. He struggled in those outings, allowing 11 runs, 10 of them earned, on 17 hits and seven walks across 13 innings. He also recorded six strikeouts.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Most of his work came in the minors. Barnes posted a 3.48 ERA with 80 strikeouts and 34 walks over 82 2/3 innings between the Triple-A affiliates of the Cubs and Dodgers. He brings a five-pitch mix that includes a four-seam fastball, sinker, slider, changeup, and sweeper.

The signing adds another left-handed pitcher to Atlanta's organizational depth. The Braves have also retained Joey Wentz and Dylan Lee through contract tenders for 2027. They recently added left-hander Mitchell Parker after claiming him off waivers from the Washington Nationals.

Barnes will now have an opportunity to compete for a spot during Spring Training. However, his minor-league contract means he will likely need to prove himself before earning a place on Atlanta's major-league roster.

The move drew reactions from fans online. One said that the signing was "Game changing," while another declared, "World Series confirmed."

#2716. Aaron Rodgers released by Steelers after benching, scathing criticism

Aaron Rodgers has been released by the Pittsburgh Steelers, according to multiple reports.

Earlier Wednesday, Rodgers went on "The Pat McAfee" show and blasted Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy for "habits" he has displayed dating back to their time together with the Green Bay Packers (2008-18).

The Steelers announced late Tuesday night that Rodgers would be inactive for the team's game Friday, Nov. 27, against the Denver Broncos. Mason Rudolph will start at quarterback.

Rodgers, who will turn 43 next week, was in his second season with the franchise. He won four MVPs and Super Bowl 45, along with McCarthy, with the Packers and spent two seasons with the New York Jets.

"At this point, I'm not going back to Pittsburgh," Rodgers told McAfee. "That ship has definitely sailed."

Rodgers will now be subject to the waiver system and can be claimed anytime until 4 p.m. ET on Thursday, Nov. 26. If he clears waivers, he will be a free agent and can sign with any team.

Rodgers has thrown 16 touchdowns and six interceptions this season. He was 27th in success rate and 29th in expected points added per play, however.

The Steelers currently maintain the seventh and final playoff spot in the AFC postseason race. Drew Allar and Will Howard are the other quarterbacks on Pittsburgh's roster in addition to Rudolph.

Rodgers has said the 2026 season, his 22nd in the league, is his final go.

"I didn't want this to be the way that it ended," Rodgers said. "I was very clear with the ultimatum. And it wasn't a veiled ultimatum. It wasn't posturing."

He also thanked the Steelers' faithful fans for embracing him. Rodgers led the Steelers to the 2025 AFC North title in former head coach Mike Tomlin's final season.

"It was special," Rodgers said. "There's nothing like being on the sidelines listening to 'Renegade' in the fourth quarter."

Rodgers currently ranks fifth on the all-time passing yards list and third on the all-time passing touchdowns list. Only five players have thrown more passes than him in NFL history. He has the league's lowest interception rate (1.4%) ever and no quarterback has taken more sacks than Rodgers.

#2709. Top Wall Street analysts favor these 3 dividend stocks for strong returns

Investors often favor dividend stocks for their regular income and the promise of potential share price appreciation. But selecting the right dividend stocks from a huge pool of companies can be difficult.

Top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend-paying stocks, as their recommendations are backed by thorough analysis of a company's financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Devon Energy (DVN), an independent oil and natural gas exploration and production company, reported better-than-expected third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of 32 cents per share for the fourth quarter, payable December 30. Based on the fixed dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, DVN yields 2.85%.

After hosting a virtual investor meeting with management, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Devon, with a price target of $59. Mehta said that he is now more confident in management's outlook for delivering strong near-term capital returns as well as efforts to drive operational efficiencies and productivity gains.

Devon sees an opportunity to increase share repurchases, driven by solid free cash flow (FCF), contributions from the Eagle Ford divestiture and continued improvement in the balance sheet, Mehta said. Devon expects share buybacks worth more than $1 billion in Q4 2026, up from $414 million in the third quarter, backed by a strong pricing backdrop and improved capital efficiency.

Mehta, a 5-star analyst, said Devon expects about $6.3 billion of excess FCF in 2027, including about $3.5 billion of net proceeds from the Eagle Ford sale. That would enable the company to accelerate buybacks while progressing toward its goal of reducing gross debt to about $9 billion by early 2027. Mehta noted that under DVN's framework to return up to 70% of annual FCF, management favors share buybacks as it believes the stock is discounted compared to large-cap peers.

"Management expects continued capital efficiency gains from merger synergies, extended laterals, surfactant adoption and development of the newly acquired federal acreage," Mehta said.

Mehta ranks No. 876 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.1%. See Devon Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Energy Transfer (ET), is a limited partnership that operates about 140,000 miles of pipeline and associated energy infrastructure. The company recently announced third-quarter results showing net income per common unit growing about 18% year-over-year, to 33 cents. ET's cash distribution for Q3 2026 was 34.25 cents per common unit, up more than 3% year-over-year. At an annualized distribution of $1.37 per share, Energy Transfer yields more than 7%.

TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer with a price target of $25. The company delivered better-than-expected Q3 results and announced the 1.2 billion cubic feet per day (bcf/d) South Mississippi Pipeline, a 215-mile, 36-inch natural gas line linking the Perryville Hub to the Florida Gas Transmission (FGT) network, Gabelman said. The project is supported by 20-year firm transportation agreements and is expected to enter service in 2030. ET expects to ultimately own 51% of the $1.6 billion project.

The 5-star analyst said that Energy Transfer lowered its 2026 capital spending outlook to $5.5 billion (midpoint of the guidance range) from $5.75 billion due to project timing. Energy Transfer expects capex to rise to $7 billion in 2027, while Gabelman projects $5 billion in both 2028 and 2029, based on disclosed projects.

Gabelman also highlighted management's commentary about optimization initiatives contributing an incremental $800 million to 2026 earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization, a larger benefit than in recent years. "A return to less commodity volatility should the [Middle East] war end could bring some relative downside, though this is offset by continued strong growth prospects," he said.

Gabelman ranks No. 397 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.4%. See Energy Transfer KPIs on TipRanks.

Oil and gas exploration and production company ConocoPhillips (COP) delivered better-than-expected Q3 performance. The company announced an 11% hike in its quarterly dividend to 93 cents per share, effective with the Q4 dividend payable on December 1. Based on the dividends paid over the trailing 12 months, COP yields about 2.6%.

Following the Q3 results, Wells Fargo analyst Sam Margolin repeated a buy rating on ConocoPhillips, citing strong potential for further dividend increases. Margolin also trimmed his price target to $185 from $189 to reflect timing effects and the WAHA forward curve, though he said that COP could still re-rate as its natural portfolio lifecycle provides stronger protection against inflationary pressures than peers. The Waha forward curve tracks future natural gas prices based on the West Texas Waha hub.

Margolin, another 5-star analyst, highlighted that ConocoPhillips' Q3 2026 adjusted earnings per share of $3.27 was well above Wells Fargo and the Street's estimates of $3.04 and $3.05, respectively. Earnings were driven by solid price realizations, partially offset by higher depletion, depreciation and amortization and purchased commodities.

Margolin also noted COP's dividend increase and said the accelerated buyback enabled by current commodity prices could further support long-term dividend growth. He sees the possibility of ConocoPhillips' dividend growth rate exceeding its current 6% cost of equity into the end of the decade. Margolin expects COP to sustain an 8% dividend compound annual growth rate through 2029 while repurchasing $6.4 billion of stock annually, and reducing net debt-to-capital to less than 9% by the end of 2028 based on the assumption of an average Brent crude oil price of $75 a barrel.

"The message on the 3Q call was similar to that of 2Q, reiterating the moderation in reinvestment rate and commitment to dividend growth through the Willow start-up and after," Margolin said.

Margolin ranks No. 189 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 69% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.6%. See ConocoPhillips Ownership Structure on TipRanks.
